Log: the total return of a relevant benchmark, by a notable margin over the coming 12 months, with shares rising in price on an absolute basis. **** * 4-STARS (Buy): Total return is expected to outperform the total return of a relevant benchmark over the coming 12 months. ** * * * 3-STARS (Hold): Total return is expected to closely approximate the total return of a relevant benchmark over the coming 12 months. ***** 2-STARS (Sell): Total return is expected to underperform the total return of a relevant benchmark over the coming 12 months. **** * 1-STAR (Strong Sell): Total return is expected to underperform the total return of a relevant benchmark by a notable margin over the coming 12 months, with shares falling in price on an absolute basis. Relevant benchmarks: In North America, the relevant benchmark is the S&P 500 Index, in Europe and in Asia, the relevant benchmarks are the MSCI AC Europe Index and the MSCI AC Asia Pacific Index, respectively. Disclosures Stocks are ranked in accordance with the following ranking methodologies: STARS Stock Reports: Qualitative STARS rankings are determined and assigned by equity analysts. For reports containing STARS rankings refer to the Glossary section of the report for detailed methodology and the definition of STARS rankings. Quantitative Stock Reports: Quantitative rankings are determined by ranking a universe of common stocks based on 5 measures or model categories: Valuation, Quality, Growth, Street Sentiment, and Price Momentum. In the U.S., a sixth sub-category for Financial Health will also be displayed. Percentile scores are used to compare each company to all other companies in the same universe for each model category. The five (six) model category scores are then weighted and rolled up into a single percentile ranking for that company. For reports containing quantitative rankings refer to the Glossary section seof the report for detailed methodology and the definition of Quantitative rankings. Analysis prepared by Keven Young on Jul 15, 2022 03:53 PM ET, when the stock traded at USD 93.89 Highlights Investment Rationale/Risk \\U2022 We see sales rising 12% in FY 22 (Oct.) and 14% in FY 23 after a 34% increase in FY 21. In Semiconductor Systems (increased 12% in the Q2'22], we see demand led by foundry/logic customers and higher investments at trailing nodes. We think revenue from Applied Global Services has further upside (~92% retention rate), with an ongoing shift towards subscriptions, which represents about 60% of segment revenue. Its Display business is seeing more modest growth (up 1% in Q2'22), aided by rising mobile adoption for OLED in 5G/foldable displays. We are encouraged by AMAT's product launch of VeritySEM systems, which are high resolution, low energy eBeam inspection systems. \\U2022 We see gross margins moderating at 47% in FY 22 and returning to 47.5% FY 23 versus 47.5% in FY 21 and 45% margin in FY 20. We anticipate supply environment continues to result in higher input costs with incremental improvement in Q4. We see AMAT investing aggressively in next-generation tools. \\U2022 We positively view FCF potential, $5.8B for FY 22 and $7B in FY 23, compared to $4.8B FCF in FY 21. We note cash of $3.9B and favorably view AMAT's commitment to return at least 80% to 100% of annual FCF to investors over the long term. \\U2022 Our Strong Buy reflects our view of improving fundamentals and a valuation aligned with historical levels when an elevated multiple is warranted. We think demand continues to be driven by foundry/logic technology transitions as well as memory capacity growth, benefiting purchases for tools in areas like etch and deposition, which possesses the greater dollar content in its business. We see AMAT's Services business benefiting from a growing installed base and higher demand for trailing node geometries and we like exposure to recurring revenue. We also see upside to estimates driven by foundry/logic capacity expansions evident in large customer \\U0441\\U0430\\U0440\\U0435\\U0445 guidances (INTC, TSMC, Samsung, and more) but note the supply constrained environment will likely continued through FY 22 \\U2022 Risks to our recommendation include greater competitive threats, a recession in the global economy in 2023 or 2024, or an inability to source components. \\U2022 Our 12-month target of $145, 16.7x our CY 23 EPS estimate of $8.65, is near large semi-equip peers. We think AMAT continues to be a share taker in areas like etch, selective removal, deposition, CMP, inspection, and process control (eBeam systems) as EUV adoption continues. Analyst's Risk Assessment LOW MEDIUM HIGH Our risk assessment reflects the historical cyclicality of the semiconductor equipment industry, a lack of visibility in the intermediate term, the dynamic nature of changes in semiconductor technology, and intense competition. This is offset by AMAT's market leadership, size, and its solid balance sheet. Business Summary May 20, 2022 CORPORATE OVERVIEW. Applied Materials (AMAT) is the worldwide leader in the manufacturing of semiconductor capital equipment. AMAT divides its business into three segments: Semiconductor Systems Group, Applied Global Services, and Display and Adjacent. The Semiconductor Systems Group, which accounted for 70% of FY 21 sales, is focused on developing and selling equipment for use in the front end of the semiconductor fabrication process. This segment develops, manufactures, and sells a range of manufacturing equipment used to fabricate semiconductor chips. This segment includes equipment used for various steps of the chip making process, including the transfer of patterns into device structures, transistor and interconnect fabrication, metrology, inspection and review, and packaging technologies for connecting finished IC die. This segment's patterning systems and technologies address challenges resulting from shrinking pattern dimensions and the complexity in vertical stacking found in semiconductor devices. Its transistor and interconnect products and technologies enable continued power and performance improvements of 3D transistors. AMAT's metrology, inspection and review systems' imaging capabilities and algorithms employ optical and e-beam technologies to meet technical demands in areas, including self-aligned double and quad patterning, extreme ultraviolet layers, measurement-intensive optimal proximity correction mask qualification, and new 3D architectures. Its packaging technologies address challenges resulting from the increasing heterogeneous integration of multiple IC dies in a single package. The company delivers capabilities that enable chipmakers to establish statistical process control, ramp up production runs, and achieve high production yields. The company's equipment is sold to integrated device manufacturers and foundries worldwide. The Applied Global Services segment, which represented 22% of FY 21 sales, provides solutions to optimize and increase productivity at customers' fabs (semiconductor fabrication facilities). The segment includes products and services to improve the efficiency and reduce operating costs at semiconductor, display, and solar customer factories. Applied Global Services products consist of spares, services, certain earlier generation products, and remanufactured equipment. Display and Adjacent markets, which accounted for 7% of FY 21 sales, develops equipment for the fabrication of flat panel displays. The Display and Adjacent Markets segment is comprised of products for manufacturing liquid crystal displays (LCDs), organic light-emitting diodes (OLEDs), and other display technologies for TVs, monitors, laptops, personal computers (PCs), electronic tablets, smartphones, and other consumer-oriented devices as well as equipment for processing flexible substrates. While similarities exist between the technologies utilized in semiconductor and display fabrication, the most significant differences are in the size and composition of the substrate. Substrates used to manufacture display panels, and other devices are typically glass, although newer flexible materials are entering the market. Display and Adjacent Markets industry growth depends primarily on consumer demand for increasingly larger and more advanced TVs and high resolution displays for mobile devices as well as new form factors, including thin, light, curved and flexible displays, and new applications such as augmented and virtual reality. CORPORATE STRATEGY. AMAT intends to use its economies of scale and worldwide distribution and support capability to fend off competition and grow market share. We think AMAT has been successful in leveraging its size and what we see as its broad expertise across segments, with limited exceptions in certain niche segments. We think AMAT benefits from a strong track record of quality products and reliable service, making it difficult for competitors to take market share. In our view, AMAT will continue to pursue acquisitions that will allow it to leverage its knowledge and expertise into new areas. MARKET PROFILE. The semiconductor industry is driven by demand for advanced electronic products, including smartphones and other mobile devices, servers, personal computers, automotive electronics, storage, and other products. The growth of data and emerging end-market drivers such as artificial intelligence, the Internet of Things, 5G networks, smart vehicles, and augmented and virtual reality are also CFRA Corporate information vestor conta Sullivan (408 727 555 Office 3050 Bowers Avenue, PO Box 58039, Santa Clara, California, 95052-3299 Telephone 408 727 5555 Fax N/A Website www.appliedmaterials.com Officers Senior VP & CFO President, CEO & R. Applied offers a variety of differentiated products that must continuously evolve to satisfy customers' requirements to compete effectively in the marketplace. There are a number of competitors serving the semiconductor manufacturing equipment industry, with some offering a single product line and others offering multiple product lines. FINANCIAL TRENDS. AMAT's business has historically been highly cyclical. In periods of strong growth in demand for semiconductors, the chipmakers optimistically extrapolate growth, leading to over-investment in equipment, which subsequently leads to a period of reduced equipment purchases. We think AMAT is less cyclical than many of its peers due to its size and product diversification. Redistribution or reproduction is prohibited without prior written permission. CFRA Sub-Industry Outlook Industry Performance Our fundamental outlook for the semiconductor equipment sub-industry for the next 12 months is positive. We believe foundry customers are sustaining increasingly elevated levels of investment, while memory customers face larger headwinds (softening demand & lower ASPs) and are more likely to cut capex. We see chip equipment sales growing by 15% in '22 after rising approximately 25% in '21. Political drivers have also emerged as a potential catalyst. With China controlling an increasing amount of the supply chain, the U.S. and Europe are both looking to boost their own chip manufacturing presence, which we believe will be done via smaller and less efficient fabs, boosting Wafer Fabrication Equipment (WFE) capital intensity and benefiting equipment suppliers. We estimate that memory spending will likely account for about 40% to 50% of total revenue in '22 and believe memory spending in 2023 falls to the lower end of this range. We believe memory market is likely to roll over in average selling prices for both DRAM & NAND in 2H '22. We believe previous consolidation in the memory space will allow customers to better sustain a healthy long- term industry landscape and create more predictable cyclical corrections moving forward. We also believe that slight capex cuts from memory firms will ultimately provide a more balanced & healthy semiconductor market, despite near term headwinds for equipment. In addition, we think China's desire to grow its local semiconductor manufacturing industry is supporting the demand for equipment. Foundry spending through '22 will likely be driven by 7-nm & below investments, as customers add capacity on these advanced technology nodes. We estimate spending by foundry manufacturers will likely grow by more than 20+% in '22, driven by aggressive spending initiatives from industry leaders Taiwan Semiconductor ($40B - $44B in '22 vs $25B - $28B in '21) as well as Intel's upgraded capital expenditures guidance ($25B- $28B in '22 vs. $18B-$19B in '21), and Samsung planning to triple its foundry capacity by '26. Foundries' share in total capital expenditures should be about 45% to 50% over the next year, based on our estimates. Semiconductor equipment sales are highly concentrated, with Intel, Taiwan Semiconductor, and Samsung expected to comprise ~60% of total sales in the coming year. We also note that equipment providers exposed to the flat panel display industry should also benefit from greater penetration for Organic LED (OLED) displays in long-term, offset by softening consumer demand in 2H '22. Long term, we see rising orders supported by the emergence of new products as well as improving demand for devices such as computers and handsets. We think customers are placing orders on advanced machinery at lower technology nodes, and we believe the release of lower-priced/more efficient mobile devices should provide additional growth. For the most part, we believe balance sheets from the largest equipment providers are healthy and could sustain pronounced cyclical downturns similar to those seen in the past. For 21 and 22, we expect free cash flow to increase, which will support greater share repurchases and higher dividend payments to maximize shareholder value. YTD through April 16, 2022, the S&P Semiconductor Equipment Index declined 33.8%, versus a drawdown of 20.5% for the S&P 1500. For the twelve months through 2021, the SSP Semiconductor Equipment Index rose 52.3% versus an increase of 26.7% for the SSP 1500. / Keven Young GICS Sector: Information Technology Sub-Industry: Semiconductor Equipment Based on SSP 1500 Indexes Five-Year market price performance through Aug 13, 2022 NasdaqGS:AMAT - Sub-Industry. Analyst Research Notes and other Company News CFRA 03:31 PM ET... CFRA Maintains Strong Buy Recommendation on Shares of Applied Materials (AMAT 93.25*****): We cut our 12-month target to $145 from $177 on a P/E of 16.7x our CY 23 EPS view of $8.65, near large semi-equip peers. We cut our FY 22 EPS estimate to $7.64 from $8.10 and lower FY 23 EPS view to $8.52 from $9.33. Our earnings revisions are a result of higher operating costs in the form of labor and expedited shipping expenses due to the difficulty of operating in a tough supply constrained environment. We are encouraged by certain transitory input costs abating and applied deploying certain price adjustments to offset higher costs in coming quarters. Long-term secular drivers for semi-equip remain in place, numerous legislative catalysts incentivizing countries to localize chip manufacturing and the building of recurring revenue streams for chip equipment manufacturers in form of services contracts due to the value add to customers from intelligent equipment. We expect AMAT to continue to return 80-100% of FCF to shareholders in form of buybacks & dividends, forecasting $5.8B in FY 22 & $7B in FY 23. / Keven Young 06:55 AM ET... CFRA Maintains Strong Buy Recommendation on Shares of Applied Materials (AMAT 108.45*****): We keep our 12-month target of $177, 19x our FY 23 (Oct.) EPS view, near peers. We cut our FY 22 EPS estimate to $8.10 from $8.15 and raise our FY 23 EPS view to $9.33 from $8.88. AMAT prints Q2 EPS of $1.85 vs $1.63, missing consensus by $0.05 due to supply constraints & Covid-19 related shutdowns. AMAT posted $6.25B in sales up 12% Y/Y driven by 15% increase in Applied Global Services (AGS) sales ($1.38B], 12% rise in System sales ($4.46B], and 1.6% growth in Display & Adjacent Markets ($381M]. We are encouraged by AGS performance expanding operating margin 400 bp Y/Y due to increased fab utilization & favorable mix, an 8% increase ( >40k) systems install base, an 11% rise in systems under subscription agreements (average tenure of 2.3 years) and a renewal rate of 92% in Q2. In addition, we positively view $1.8B in repurchases (approving new $6B program) and $211M in dividends in Q2 and we expect 80%-100% of FCF to be returned to shareholders in long term. We see FCF of $6.8B in FY 22 and $7.6B in FY 23. / Keven Young December 14, 2021 02:47 PM ET... CFRA Upgrades Shares of Applied Materials to Strong Buy from Buy (AMAT 147.47*****): We keep our 12-month price target of $175, 20x our FY 23 (Oct.) EPS estimate, above peers to reflect our view of AMAT's market share lead position. We maintain our FY 22 EPS estimate of $8.00 and our FY 23 EPS estimate of $8.75. We see upside to estimates driven by two trends: 1) AMAT continuing to gain market share in process control, more specifically wafer inspection and e-beam technology with leading-edge customers, and 2) demand from foundry/ logic capacity expansions evident in Intel's higher capex guidance ($25B - $28B in '22 vs. $18B - $19B in '21) and Samsung's plans to triple its foundry capacity by 2026. We also see demand supported by the U.S. and Europe looking to boost their own chip manufacturing presences, which will likely be done via smaller and less efficient fabs, boosting WFE capital intensity and benefitting equipment suppliers. We are slightly tempered in our expectations for AMAT as it continues to face issues sourcing components for equipment sales. / Keven Young November 19, 2021 07:21 AM ET... CFRA Maintains Buy Recommendation on Shares of Applied Materials, Inc. (AMAT 150.65****): We raise our 12-month target by $25 to $175, 20x revised FY 23 (Oct.) EPS estimate of $8.75, above peers to reflect our view of AMAT's market share lead position. We increase our FY 22 EPS estimate to $8.00 from $7.89 and FY 23 to $8.75 from $8.18. AMAT posted Oct-Q EPS of $1.94 vs $1.25, missing the consensus by $0.02 due to component constraints. Sales rose 34% Y/Y, led by growth of 43% in Semiconductor Systems and 21% in Applied Global Services (AGS), while Display & Adjacent Markets was up a more modest 2%. Despite its Oct-Q revenue miss ($6.1B actual vs $6.3B consensus), AMAT posted record backlog of $11.8B. Within Semiconductor Systems, we see demand driven by foundry/logic capacity expansion and customer shift from just-in-time to just-in-case inventory management. Within AGS, we like that nearly 15k installed base tools are covered by agreements (up 12% Y/Y], with the tenure of these agreements at 2.3 years (vs 1.9 years at end-2020) and a renewal rate of about 90%. We note net cash position of $2.1B. / Keven Young October 15, 2021 11:53 AM ET... CFRA Upgrades Shares of Applied Materials to Buy from Hold (AMAT 132.17****): We keep our 12-month target at $150 on a P/E of 18.8x our CY 22 estimate of $7.96, above peers to reflect our view of AMAT's market share lead position. We maintain our FY 21 (Oct.) EPS estimate of $6.85 and FY 22 at $7.89, representing 15% growth YoY after a 74% rise in FY 21. Our upgrade to buy is primarily driven by our view for rising demand from foundry/logic customers in its transition from FinFET to gate-all-around, memory capacity growth, and improving fundamentals We see AMAT's process control business gaining traction as well as the company's wafer inspection and e-beam technology business from leading-edge customers. We see AMAT's higher margin services business gaining traction, driven by increased demand for trailing end nodes. We see AMAT as less cyclical than its semiconductor equipment peers due to its product diversification and increased demand from China. Although we anticipate AMAT will return above 80% of annual FCF to investors, we view a potential peak in memory prices as a risk. / Keven Young 02:59 PM ET... CFRA Maintains Strong Buy Recommendation on Shares of Applied Materials (AMAT 116.71*****): We keep our 12-month target of $177, 20x our FY 23 (Oct.) EPS view, in line with peers. We maintain our FY 22 EPS view of $8.15 and keep our FY 23 EPS of $8.88. We attended AMAT's Master Class \\\"New Ways to Shrink,\\\" where they shined a light on the adoption of EUV lithography. We highlighted the importance of this technology inflection recently (\\\"Chip Equipment Beneficiaries from Continued Adoption of EUV\\\" Thematic) and positively view AMAT'S TAM growth opportunities (> $1B increase in TAM per 100k wafer starts) with transition from FinFET transistors to Gate-All-Around (GAA) being enabled by ability to print smaller patterns. We also like AMAT's product launch of VeritySEM systems, which are new high-resolution, low-power eBeam inspection systems, as legacy optical inspection systems are not equipped to deal with defects on EUV devices (EUV device features are >10x smaller). In addition, we see AMAT positioned to take share in DRAM/logic patterning market (etch & selective removal) with EUV adoption. / Keven Young February 16, 2022 05:39 PM ET... AMAT remains supply constrained adding $1.3B in system sales backlog in Q1'22 ($8B backlog), we view this favorably as supply constraints begin to ease in '22. Glossary STARS Since January 1, 1987, CFRA Equity and Fund Research Services, and its predecessor S&P Capital IQ Equity Research has ranked a universe of U.S common stocks, ADRs (American Depositary Receipts), and ADSs (American Depositary Shares) based on a given equity's potential for future performance. Similarly, we have ranked Asian and European equities since June 30, 2002 Under proprietary STARS (Stock Appreciation Ranking System), equity analysts rank equities according to their individual forecast of an equity's future total return potential versus the expected total return of a relevant benchmark (e.g., a regional index (MSCI AC Asia Pacific Index, MSCI AC Europe Index or S&P 500\\U00ae Index]], based on a 12-month time horizon. STARS was designed to help investors looking to put their investment decisions in perspective. Data used to assist in determining the STARS ranking may be the result of the analyst's own models as well as internal proprietary models resulting from dynamic data inputs. S&P Global Market Intelligence's Quality Ranking (also known as S&P Capital IQ Earnings & Dividend Rankings) - Growth and SSP Capital IQ Earnings & Dividend Rankings stability of earnings and dividends are deemed key elements in establishing S&P Global Market Intelligence's earnings and dividend rankings for common stocks, which are designed to capsulize the nature of this record in a single symbol. It should be noted, however, that the process also takes into consideration certain adjustments and modifications deemed desirable in establishing such rankings. The final score for each stock is measured against a scoring matrix determined by analysis of the scores of a large and representative sample of stocks. The range of scores in the array of this sample has been aligned with the following ladder of rankings: A+ Highest High A Above B C B+ Average Below Average B- Lower Lowest D In Reorganization NC Not Ranked EPS Estimates CFRA's earnings per share (EPS] estimates reflect analyst projections of future EPS from continuing operations, and generally exclude various items that are viewed as special, non-recurring, or extraordinary. Also, EPS estimates reflect either forecasts of equity analysts; or, the consensus (average) EPS estimate, which are independently compiled by S&P Global Market Intelligence, a data provider to CFRA. Among the items typically excluded from EPS estimates are asset sale gains; impairment, restructuring or merger-related charges; legal and insurance settlements; in process research and development expenses; gains or losses on the extinguishment of debt; the cumulative effect of accounting changes; and earnings related to operations that have been classified by the company as discontinued. The inclusion of some items, such CFRA 12-Month Target Price The equity analyst's projection of the market price a given security will command 12 months hence, based on a combination of intrinsic, relative, anc orivate market valuation metrics, including Fair Value. Abbreviations Used in Equity Research Reports CAGR - Compound Annual Growth Rate CAPEX - Capital Expenditures CY - Calendar Year DCF - Discounted Cash Flow DDM - Dividend Discount Model EBIT - Earnings Before Interest and Taxes EBITDA - Earnings Before Interest, Taxes, Depreciation & Amortization EPS - Earnings Per Share EV - Enterprise Value FCF - Free Cash Flow FFO - Funds From Operations FY - Fiscal Year P/E - Price/Earnings P/NAV - Price to Net Asset Value PEG Ratio - P/E-to-Growth Ratio PV - Present Value R&D - Research & Development ROCE - Return on Capital Employed ROE Return on Equity ROl - Return on Investment ROIC - Return on Invested Capital ROA - Return on Assets SG&A - Selling, General & Administrative Expenses SOTP - Sum-of-The-Parts WACC - Weighted Average Cost of Capital Dividends on American Depository Receipts (ADRs) and American Depository Shares (ADSs) are net of taxes (paid in the country of origin). Reflects an equity analyst's view of a given company's operational risk, or the risk of a firm's ability to continue as an ongoing concern. The Qualitative Risk Assessment is a relative ranking to the U.S. STARS universe, and should be reflective of risk factors related to a company's operations, as opposed to risk and volatility measures associated with share prices. For an ETF this reflects on a capitalization-weighted basis, the average qualitative risk assessment assigned to holdings of the fund. STARS Ranking system and definition: **** * 5-STARS (Strong Buy): Total return is expected to outperform the total return of a relevant benchmark, by a notable margin over the coming 12 months, with shares rising in price on an absolute basis. **** * 4-STARS (Buy): Total return is expected to outperform the total return of a relevant benchmark over the coming 12 months. **** * 3-STARS (Hold): Total return is expected to closely approximate the total return of a relevant benchmark over the coming 12 months. ***** 2-STARS (Sell): Total return is expected to underperform the total return of a relevant benchmark over the coming 12 months. **** * 1-STAR (Strong Sell): Total return is expected to underperform the total return of a relevant benchmark by a notable margin over the coming 12 months, with shares falling Relevant benchmarks: In North America, the relevant benchmark is the S&P 500 Index, in Europe and in Asia, the relevant benchmarks are the MSCI AC Europe Index and the MSCI AC Asia Pacific Index, respectively. Disclosures Stocks are ranked in accordance with the following ranking methodologies: STARS Stock Reports: Qualitative STARS rankings are determined and assigned by equity analysts. For reports containing STARS rankings refer to the Glossary section of the report for detailed methodology and the definition of STARS rankings. Quantitative Stock Reports: Quantitative rankings are determined by ranking a universe of common stocks based on 5 measures or model categories: Valuation, Quality, Growth, Street Sentiment, and Price Momentum. In the U.S., a sixth sub-category for Financial Health will also be displayed. Percentile scores are used to compare each company to all other companies in the same universe for each model category. The five (six) model category scores are then weighted and rolled up into a single percentile ranking for that company. For reports containing quantitative rankings refer to the Glossary section seof the report for detailed methodology and the definition of Quantitative rankings. STARS Stock Reports and Quantitative Stock Reports: The methodologies used in STARS Stock Reports and Quantitative Stock Reports (collectively, the \\\"Research Reports\\\") reflect different criteria, assumptions and analytical methods and may have differing rankings. The methodologies and data used to generate the different types of Research Reports are believed by the author and distributor reasonable and appropriate. Generally, CFRA does not generate reports with different ranking methodologies for the same issuer. However, in the event that different methodologies or data are used on the analysis of an issuer, the methodologies may lead to different views on the issuer, which may at times result in contradicting assessments of an issuer. CFRA reserves the right to alter, replace or vary models, methodologies or assumptions from time to time and without notice to clients. Analysis prepared by Jun Zhang Tan, CFA on Jul 21, 2022 02:42 AM ET, when the stock traded at USD 514.00. Highlights Investment Rationale/Risk \\U2022 ASML's 02 2022 net sales of EUR5.4 billion were slightly ahead of its guidance, but we find it impressive that its bookings were strong at record quarterly of EUR8.5 billion, including EUR5.4 billion of EUV and High NA systems. Despite some weakness in certain end markets, the overall demand for ASML's system remains strong driven by node migration. ASML is still expecting to ship 55 EUV systems in 2022, but it will only recognize 40 instead of previous target of 49 due to the effect of fast shipments resulting in revenue deferral. 2022 net sales growth guidance is reduced from 20% to 10% due to EUR2.8 billion delay in revenue recognition, but this also implies upsides for 2023 net sales. \\U2022 Q2 2022 EBIT of EUR1.6 billion (+33% Y/Y) was slightly ahead of consensus estimates. However, ASML's Q3 2022 implied EBIT guidance of EUR1.5 billion (-20% Y/Y) is 37% below consensus estimates. Due to the revenue deferral related to high margin EUV systems and cost inflation, ASML expects 2022 gross margin to be 49-50% vs. previous guidance of 52%. This implies high teens cut in consensus EBIT estimates. \\U2022 We adjust our 2022 and 2023 EPS to EUR14.50 (EUR17.51) and EUR21.50 (EUR20.50) to reflect the guidance. \\U2022 ASML is in the monopoly position in supplying EUV tools to both logic and memory clients. As ASML's clients are expanding the capacity to meet the demands and migrating to more advanced nodes, this should continue to drive ASML's sales in the medium term. The cost inflation and revenue deferral headwinds will drag 2022 gross margin to 49-50% level, but we believe that the focus should be on the strong bookings for its advanced EUV systems. A positive revenue mix will lead to a sustainable gross margin. More important, ASML is studying feasibility to increase its 2025 capacity production for low and high NA EUV to 90 and 20 units, above its previous CMD targets and consensus expectations, suggesting strong medium-term outlook remains intact. \\U2022 Downside risks include: (i) extent of EUV adoption being less than expected, (ii) slower economic growth, which impacts semiconductor demand, and (iii) geopolitical tension. \\U2022 We lower our target price for ASML to USD755 (from USD892), representing a 35x 2022 EV/ EBITDA. The valuation is a premium to its five- year historical average of 24x, justified in our view by its monopoly position and the accelerating adoption of its EUV systems. Analyst's Risk Assessment LOW MEDIUM HIGH Our risk assessment reflects the historical cyclicality of the semiconductor equipment industry, the lack of visibility in the medium term, the dynamic nature of changes in semiconductor technology, and intense competition. These risks are only partly offset by the company's strong market position, size, and financial condition. Business Summary Jan 24, 2022 COMPANY BACKGROUND: Headquartered in Veldhoven, ASML Holding N.V. was created initially as a joint venture between Philips and Advanced Semiconductor Materials International in 1984. Today, ASML is a world-leading semiconductor equipment company with more than 14,000 employees and counting all of the top chipmakers as its customers. It is primarily involved in the design, manufacturing, and marketing and servicing of photolithography systems. The company's shares are listed on both Euronext Amsterdam and NASDAQ, while major shareholders include Capital Group International (15.28%) and BlackRock (7.69%) as of PRODUCTS OVERVIEW: The company's key products - photolithography systems - are essential to the chips manufacturing process in which light is used to project circuit patterns onto silicon wafers coated with light-sensitive materials, thereby engraving or enabling the formation of the exposed patterns on the wafers. To create more powerful chips, an increasing amount of transistors has to be crammed onto the wafers, and it is imperative that the size of the transistors has to be shrunk. This is limited by the wavelength of the light source utilized in the systems. The development of Extreme Ultraviolet (EUV) lithography technology is ASML's answer to the challenge of sub-10 nm manufacturing whereby a EUV light source with a wavelength of only 13.5 nm is utilized. ASML sees a healthy semiconductor end-market growth fueled by major innovation drivers, such as 5G connectivity, artificial intelligence, autonomous driving, and big data. This translates into growth of worldwide capacity in all segments, especially at the leading-edge nodes, creating opportunities for ASML that is near-monopoly in advanced lithography MARKETS OVERVIEW: ASML serves two main groups of customers with different end-market - Memory and Logic. The first customer group is involved in the manufacturing of memory chips, which are used to store data and could be further divided into NAND-Flash and DRAM, whereby the former could retain data without power and is increasingly used to replace magnetic storage media (primarily hard disks), while the latter primarily serve to enhance the performance of electronic products. Logic chips (or microprocessors), on the other hand, function as information processors in electronic devices. Manufacturers that both design and manufacture microprocessors are referred to as Integrated Device Manufacturers (IDM], while those specialized only in the manufacturing process are known as foundries. In 2021, ASMLs net bookings increased to EUR26.24 billion (EUR11.29 billion), driven by both memory and logic customers. In addition to the structural demand for EUV systems, ASML also benefitted from capacity constraints faced by the global semiconductor industry. COMPETITIVE LANDSCAPE: ASML has a clear technology lead over peers, in our view, which should allow the company to gain market share in the semiconductor equipment sector and insulate it to some degree from industry cyclicality. The company has more than 80% share of the global lithography equipment market and is dominant in the high-end segments, while major competitors - Canon and Nikon - lead in the low-end segments such as i-line and ArF technology. In view of the significant capital investments required to develop new lithography technology, we believe ASML will remain ahead of its competitors with its EUV development, which received backings from major customers, including Intel, Samsung, and TSMC through a co-investment program. FINANCIAL TRENDS: ASML operates in a highly cyclical industry supplying a small number of customers and hence its financial performance is volatile. During the period 2017-2021, ASML's revenue has grown at a CAGR of 20%, driven by growth in net system sales and net services and field option sales. The gross margin has increased from 44.9% to 52.7% during the same period, thanks to product mix improvement. We expect the margin to improve further as the contribution of higher-ASP EUV systems increase in the future. We note that the proportion of group revenues from services has increased, driven by the growing installed base of lithography machines. This will help to stabilize its earnings going forward as we believe service revenues are far more resilient to down cvcles in the semiconductor industry. With ASML's roadmap to ship next- generation EUV being on track, we think this should help in ASML's pricing and gross margin. ASML's EBIT has grown at an impressive CAGR of 29% during 2017-2021 and we see room for margin expansion going forward. In its recent CMD, ASML is targeting EUR24-30 billion revenue and gross margin of 54-56% in 2025, which we believe are achievable. Sub-Industry Outlook Our fundamental outlook for the semiconductor equipment sub-industry for the next 12 months is positive. We believe foundry customers are sustaining increasingly elevated levels of investment, while memory customers face larger headwinds (softening demand & lower ASPs) and are more likely to cut capex. We see chip equipment sales growing by 15% in '22 after rising approximately 25% in '21. Political drivers have also emerged as a potential catalyst. With China controlling an increasing amount of the supply chain, the U.S. and Europe are both looking to boost their own chip manufacturing presence, which we believe will be done via smaller and less efficient fabs, boosting Wafer Fabrication Equipment (WFE) capital intensity and benefiting equipment suppliers. We estimate that memory spending will likely account for about 40% to 50% of total revenue in '22 and believe memory spending in 2023 falls to the lower end of this range. We believe memory market is likely to roll over in average selling prices for both DRAM & NAND in 2H '22. We believe previous consolidation in the memory space will allow customers to better sustain a healthy long- term industry landscape and create more predictable cyclical corrections moving forward. We also believe that slight capex cuts from memory firms will ultimately provide a more balanced & healthy semiconductor market, despite near term headwinds for equipment. In addition, we think China's desire to grow its local semiconductor manufacturing industry is supporting the demand for equipment. Foundry spending through '22 will likely be driven by 7-nm & below investments, as customers add capacity on these advanced technology nodes. We estimate spending by foundry manufacturers will likely grow by more than 20+% in '22, driven by aggressive spending initiatives from industry leaders Taiwan Semiconductor ($40B - $44B in '22 vs $25B - $28B in '21) as well as Intel's upgraded capital expenditures guidance ($25B- $28B in '22 vs. $18B-$19B in '21), and Samsung planning to triple its foundry capacity by '26. Foundries' share in total capital expenditures should be about 45% to 50% over the next year, based on our estimates. Semiconductor equipment sales are highly concentrated, with Intel, Taiwan Semiconductor, and Samsung expected to comprise ~60% of total sales in the coming year. We also note that equipment providers exposed to the flat panel display industry should also benefit from greater penetration for Organic LED (OLED) displays in long-term, offset by softening consumer demand in 2H '22. Long term, we see rising orders supported by the emergence of new products as well as improving demand for devices such as computers and handsets. We think customers are placing orders on advanced machinery at lower technology nodes, and we believe the release of lower-priced/more efficient mobile devices should provide additional growth. For the most part, we believe balance sheets from the largest equipment providers are healthy and could sustain pronounced cyclical downturns similar to those seen in the past. For 21 and 22, we expect free cash flow to increase, which will support greater share repurchases and higher dividend payments to maximize shareholder value. YTD through April 16, 2022, the S&P Semiconductor Equipment Index declined 33.8%, versus a drawdown of 20.5% for the S&P 1500. For the twelve months through 2021, the SSP Semiconductor Equipment Index rose 52.3% versus an increase of 26.7% for the SSP 1500. / Keven Young Sub-Industry: Semiconductor Equipment Peer Group*: Semiconductor Equipment Recent Stock Price 561.30 22.11 108.27 66.73 294.00 105.47 382.02 503.15 112.55 317.22 100.93 CFRA Industry Performance GICS Sector: Information Technology Sub-Industry: Semiconductor Equipment Based on SSP 1500 Indexes Five-Year market price performance through Aug 20, 2022 NasdaqGS:ASML - Sub-Industry S&P 1500 480% 400% 320% * 240% 160% + 80% - 2018 2019 2020 2021 2022 NOTE: A sector chart appears when the sub-industry does not have sufficient historical index data. Glossary STARS Since January 1, 1987, CFRA Equity and Fund Research Services, and its predecessor S&P Capital IQ Equity Research has ranked a universe of U.S common stocks, ADRs (American Depositary Receipts), and ADSs (American Depositary Shares) based on a given equity's potential for future performance. Similarly, we have ranked Asian and European equities since June 30, 2002 Under proprietary STARS (Stock Appreciation Ranking System), equity analysts rank equities according to their individual forecast of an equity's future total return potential versus the expected total return of a relevant benchmark (e.g., a regional index (MSCI AC Asia Pacific Index, MSCI AC Europe Index or S&P 500\\U00ae Index]], based on a 12-month time horizon. STARS was designed to help investors looking to put their investment decisions in perspective. Data used to assist in determining the STARS ranking may be the result of the analyst's own models as well as internal proprietary models resulting from dynamic data inputs. S&P Global Market Intelligence's Quality Ranking (also known as S&P Capital IQ Earnings & Dividend Rankings) - Growth and SSP Capital IQ Earnings & Dividend Rankings stability of earnings and dividends are deemed key elements in establishing S&P Global Market Intelligence's earnings and dividend rankings for common stocks, which are designed to capsulize the nature of this record in a single symbol. It should be noted, however, that the process also takes into consideration certain adjustments and modifications deemed desirable in establishing such rankings. The final score for each stock is measured against a scoring matrix determined by analysis of the scores of a large and representative sample of stocks. The range of scores in the array of this sample has been aligned with the following ladder of rankings: A+ Highest High A Above B C B+ Average Below Average B- Lower Lowest D In Reorganization NC Not Ranked EPS Estimates CFRA's earnings per share (EPS] estimates reflect analyst projections of future EPS from continuing operations, and generally exclude various items that are viewed as special, non-recurring, or extraordinary. Also, EPS estimates reflect either forecasts of equity analysts; or, the consensus (average) EPS estimate, which are independently compiled by S&P Global Market Intelligence, a data provider to CFRA. Among the items typically excluded from EPS estimates are asset sale gains; impairment, restructuring or merger-related charges; legal and insurance settlements; in process research and development expenses; gains or losses on the extinguishment of debt; the cumulative effect of accounting changes; and earnings related to operations that have been classified by the company as discontinued. The inclusion of some items, such 12-Month Target Price The equity analyst's projection of the market price a given security will command 12 months hence, based on a combination of intrinsic, relative, anc orivate market valuation metrics, including Fair Value. CFRA Abbreviations Used in Equity Research Reports CAGR - Compound Annual Growth Rate CAPEX - Capital Expenditures CY - Calendar Year DCF - Discounted Cash Flow DDM - Dividend Discount Model EBIT - Earnings Before Interest and Taxes EBITDA - Earnings Before Interest, Taxes, Depreciation & Amortization EPS - Earnings Per Share EV - Enterprise Value FCF - Free Cash Flow FFO - Funds From Operations FY - Fiscal Year P/E - Price/Earnings P/NAV - Price to Net Asset Value PEG Ratio - P/E-to-Growth Ratio PV - Present Value R&D - Research & Development ROCE - Return on Capital Employed ROE Return on Equity ROl - Return on Investment ROIC - Return on Invested Capital ROA - Return on Assets SG&A - Selling, General & Administrative Expenses SOTP - Sum-of-The-Parts WACC - Weighted Average Cost of Capital Dividends on American Depository Receipts (ADRs) and American Depository Shares (ADSs) are net of taxes (paid in the country of origin). Reflects an equity analyst's view of a given company's operational risk, or the risk of a firm's ability to continue as an ongoing concern. The Qualitative Risk Assessment is a relative ranking to the U.S. STARS universe, and should be reflective of risk factors related to a company's operations, as opposed to risk and volatility measures associated with share prices. For an ETF this reflects on a capitalization-weighted basis, the average qualitative risk assessment assigned to holdings of the fund. STARS Ranking system and definition: **** * 5-STARS (Strong Buy): Total return is expected to outperform the total return of a relevant benchmark, by a notable margin over the coming 12 months, with shares rising in price on an absolute basis. **** * 4-STARS (Buy): Total return is expected to outperform the total return of a relevant benchmark over the coming 12 months. ** * * * 3-STARS (Hold): Total return is expected to closely approximate the total return of a relevant benchmark over the coming 12 months. ***** 2-STARS (Sell): Total return is expected to underperform the total return of a relevant benchmark over the coming 12 months. **** * 1-STAR (Strong Sell): Total return is expected to underperform the total return of a relevant benchmark by a notable margin over the coming 12 months, with shares falling in price on an absolute basis. Relevant benchmarks: In North America, the relevant benchmark is the S&P 500 Index, in Europe and in Asia, the relevant benchmarks are the MSCI AC Europe Index and the MSCI AC Asia Pacific Index, respectively. Analysis prepared by Brooks Idlet, CFA on Oct 16, 2024 04:18 PM ET, when the stock traded at USD 683.52. Highlights Investment Rationale/Risk \\U2022 We see sales growth of 2% in 2024, 21% in 2025, and 13% in 2026. 2024 has been labeled a transition year, with bookings (EUR11.8B YTD through Q3) setting up solid growth in 2025, though recent delays in customer node transitions-along with evolving export restrictions with China (47% of Q3 sales) and a more gradual industry recovery than expected across multiple end markets-have pushed some sales into later years. 2025 and 2026 should see significant demand from newly constructed fabs amid the strengthening semi upcycle, helping meet growing Al demand, supported by ASML's large backlog (EUR36B). China is projected to fall to ~20% of 2025 sales as it digests sizeable purchases made in 2022 and 2023, and we see upside to these levels as mature-node demand remains strong. \\U2022 We see gross margin of 50.8% in 2024, expanding to 52.5% in 2025, vs. 51.3% in 2023. Higher prices for ASML's newest low-NA EUV systems (the NXE:3800) are expected to be offset by lower-than-expected EUV volumes and declining China sales. \\U2022 We project FCF of EUR3.8B in 2024 and EUR8.0B in 2025, following EUR3.3B in 2023. FCF is seeing pressure as ASML operates at higher inventory levels to support future advanced technology deliveries. \\U2022 Our opinion is Strong Buy, as we think recent selling pressure is an overreaction to current market softness, while ASML's sizeable backlog and market leadership position it favorably for growth once industry activity accelerates. ASML is the world's only supplier of EUV lithography systems, which are critical for leading-edge semi manufacturers to migrate to more advanced nodes, supporting applications like Al. ASML has no EUV competition and is extending its lead as it has started shipping its next-gen High-NA EUV systems (ramping in 2025). It also holds a high market share in less-advanced DUV systems for mature nodes. Longer term, the industry is set to grow from megatrends such as Al and electrification, benefiting ASML given its key role in the manufacturing process. \\U2022 Primary risks include delays in global fab construction, emerging competition in EUV that introduces pricing pressure, and an expansion of export controls to cover mature nodes (which we see as unlikely despite recent headlines). \\U2022 Our target of $902 is 30x our 2025 EPS view, below ASML's three-year average (~31x) on market uncertainty and evolving export controls, but above peers given ASML's dominant share of the litho market. Analyst's Risk Assessment LOW MEDIUM HIGH Our risk assessment reflects the historical cyclicality of the semiconductor equipment industry and the dynamic nature of changes in semiconductor technology. These risks are offset by the company's dominant market position, size, and financial condition. Business Summary Oct 16, 2024 COMPANY BACKGROUND: Headquartered in Veldhoven, Netherlands, ASML Holding N.V. was created initially as a joint venture between Philips and Advanced Semiconductor Materials International in 1984. Today, ASML is a world-leading semiconductor equipment company with more than 42,000 employees, and counts all of the top chipmakers as its customers. It is primarily involved in the design, manufacturing, and servicing of photolithography systems. The company's shares are listed on both Euronext Amsterdam and NASDAQ. PRODUCT OVERVIEW: The company's key products- photolithography systems-are essential to the chips manufacturing process. During photolithography, light is used to project patterns onto silicon wafers coated with light-sensitive materials, thereby creating patterns on the wafers. One of the key factors driving chip performance is minimum transistor size, as smaller transistors allow for more of them to fit onto a chip. Transistor shrinking has partially been aided from techniques such as projecting multiple patterns onto a wafer concurrently (\\\"patterning\\\"), but the primary innovation driver relates to the light source used. The leading-edge semiconductor manufacturers have introduced processes to make chips with transistor spacing below 3 nanometers, well below the visible light spectrum (380-740 nm). Over time, ASML has introduced the ability to reduce the wavelength of emitted light down to 193nm through its deep ultraviolet (DUV) machines, down to 13.5nm through its extreme ultraviolet (EUV) machines (a major technological breakthrough after decades of development), and most recently down to 8nm with its \\\"High-NA\\\" EUV systems. Each of ASML's EUV machines costs roughly $200M, with the company's newest High-NA EUV machines-expected to ramp in 2025-likely to cost over $350M. Despite the high price tag, each of the world's most advanced logic chipmakers (TSMC, Samsung, and Intel) have shown strong interest in this next-gen machine and have purchase orders in place. In addition to its EUV and DUV machines, ASML sells computational lithography software and metrology / inspection products that quickly identify and adjust for errors during the manufacturing process. The company also makes money through servicing and upgrading customers' existing systems. ASML sees healthy semiconductor end-market growth fueled by major innovation drivers, such as 5G connectivity, artificial intelligence, autonomous driving, and big data. MARKET OVERVIEW: ASML serves two main groups of chipmakers with different end markets: memory and logic. Memory chips (DRAM and NAND) are used to store data and enhance performance in electronic devices; logic chips (or microprocessors), on the other hand, function as information processors in those devices, acting as the brains of the device. In 2023, 73% of ASML's net system sales were to logic customers (27% memory). The logic chip market is more characterized by the constant drive towards cutting-edge, smaller-nm processes, while the memory market is generally more commoditized, but has introduced more advanced processes recently given the requirement from Al applications for advanced DRAM. Thus, high demand for EUV exists in both areas. By country of origin, ASML's 2023 sales were primarily from Taiwan (29%), China (26%), South Korea (25%), and the U.S. (11%); importantly, recent (and evolving) export restrictions have limited the company's ability to sell advanced machinery (i.e., EUV and advanced \\\"immersion\\\" DUV systems) into China, where company sales grew 149% Y/Y in 2023. China accounted for 49% of sales in both Q1 and Q2 2024, and 47% in 03. COMPETITIVE LANDSCAPE: ASML has a clear technology lead over peers, in our view, which should allow the company to gain market share in the semiconductor equipment sector and insulate it to some degree from industry cyclicality. EUV technology is required in order to produce the most advanced chips, and ASML is the only company in the world that manufactures EUV systems. The company has more than 80% share of the global lithography equipment market and is dominant in the high-end segments, while major competitors-Canon and Nikon-lead in the low-end segments such as i-line and ArF technology. Creating new lithography technology requires significant investment, and ASML has also built hard-to-replicate relationships with its suppliers; for example, it holds a large ownership stake in Germany's Zeiss Group, which manufacturers state-of-the-art optical components that are used in EUV. Using EUV allows customers to minimize defects, costs, and cycle times. Compared to using EUV, manufacturing leading edge chips with legacy technologies like immersion lithography involves a significant increase in process steps related to very large multi-patterning schemes. FINANCIAL TRENDS: ASML operates in a highly cyclical industry and supplies a small number of customers with a small number of expensive machines, leading to volatile financial performance. Still, sales growth has been consistent over longer periods, with ASML generating a revenue CAGR of 20.3% from 2018-2023. Gross margin increased from 46.0% to 51.3% during the same period, thanks to product mix improvement and higher system prices. During 03 2024, gross margin was 50.8%, down 70 bps Q/Q. We expect margins to improve slowly in 2025, as higher volumes are offset by lower-margin High-NA systems and a declining China business. ASML's service revenue, driven by the growing installed base of ASML's lithography machines, represented 20.4% of overall sales in 2023 (vs. 27.1% in 2022). Q3 service revenue was 20.6% of total sales, down from 23.7% in Q2. In our view, its services business helps stabilize results given its higher resilience to down cycles in the semiconductor industry. As of September 29, 2024, ASML had EUR5.0B in cash and short-term investments alongside EUR4.7B in debt for a net cash position of EUR292M, down from EUR411M exiting the prior quarter and EUR2.25B exiting 2023 as the company has been using cash to build inventory in anticipation of future advanced system sales. ASML is expecting to reach EUR30B to EUR35B in revenue by 2025 and EUR44B to EUR60B in revenue by 2030. Redistribution or reproduction is prohibited without prior written permission. CFRA Sub-Industry Outlook Industry Performance Our fundamental outlook for the semiconductor materials & equipment sub-industry for the next 12 months is positive. Signs continue to emerge that a semiconductor upcycle is picking up speed following a prolonged period of softness that bottomed in late 2023. The recovery is broadening, with both logic and memory expected to tick up in 2024 and advance further in 2025. We see wafer fabrication equipment spending rising 4% in 2024 and 15% in 2025 following a roughly flat year in 2023. Recent strength has been largely driven by Al demand, with semiconductor companies looking to advanced equipment producers across both logic and memory in order to manufacture more efficient Al-enabling chips and systems. The effect has primarily been seen in the data center, where we continue to expect the bulk of Al-related spending to reside in 2024. However, we see a broadening into edge Al benefiting the space in 2025, led by the release of Al-enabled smartphones, with additional demand from automotive and industrial loT applications. The memory market has shown the most dramatic increase after a historically soft period came to an end in late 2023, with utilization increasing following meaningful production cuts and lower prices. Advanced DRAM/high-bandwidth memory (HBM) has been a key driver of the rebound in memory, primarily to enable Al applications. This has also benefited back-end equipment producers, with HBM generally playing a key role in Al- enabling advanced packaging schemes. Notably, advanced memory production now utilizes EUV technology, similar to advanced logic production. We see memory equipment spending growing by 15% in 2024 and 30% in 2025 following a decline of nearly 30% in 2023, with DRAM spending growing about 25% in 2024 and 15% in 2025. We expect flattish foundry/logic equipment sales in 2024 prior to a 10%-15% bounce in 2025, largely driven by a ramp of 2-nm node-enabling equipment as the industry utilizes new technology like gate-all-around transistors. We expect advanced logic to lead the market, offset by a slight reduction in demand for trailing-edge equipment as China, the largest consumer of less-advanced semiconductor equipment, digests its recent purchases. We expect global foundry capacity to increase only slightly in 2024 and 2025 (following 5% growth in 2023) as elevated capex plans by TSMC ($31B projected in 2024, $36B in 2025) and Samsung (planning to triple foundry capacity by 2026) are partially offset by spending cuts at Intel ($23B in 2024 and $20B in 2025) given its issues with leading-edge competitiveness. Political drivers remain a catalyst, with global tensions encouraging countries to localize their semi supply chains. The US CHIPS and Science Act, for example, set aside $39B for semiconductor manufacturing (and another $11B for R&D], about $32.5B of which has been allocated as of August 31, 2024. On the other hand, geopolitical tensions also create material risks for the industry, especially for equipment makers tied to the leading edge as the U.S. and others look to impede China from reaching high-volume capabilities in advanced nodes. The most recent U.S. export restrictions were announced in October 2023, and participation from other countries continues to evolve, helping shift China's spending toward the trailing edge. YTD through October 4, the S&P Semiconductor Materials & Equipment Index has increased 15.7% vs. a 19.7% rise for the S&P 1500. In 2023, the sub-industry rose 42.2% vs. the S&P 1500's 23.4% gain. / Brooks Idlet, CFA GICS Sector: Information Technology Sub-Industry: Semiconductor Materials and Equipment Based on SSP 1500 Indexes Five-Year market price performance through Nov 09, 2024 NasdaqGS:ASML \\U2014 Sub-Industry S&P 1500 280% 240% 200% 160% Change \\U2265 120% 80% 40% 0% -40% 2020 2021 2022 2023 2024 2025 NOTE: A sector chart appears when the sub-industry does not have sufficient historical index data. ASML's backlog is now at EUR39B (+EUR1B Q/Q). / Brooks Idlet, CFA 12:01 PM ET... CFRA Maintains Strong Buy Opinion on Shares of ASML Holding N.V. (ASML 689.00*****): We keep our target at $902 (30x our 2025 EPS view) following ASML's earnings call, held today after Q3 results were released yesterday, when we adjusted our 2024 EPS view to EUR19.12 (+EUR0.06), 2025's to EUR27.58 (-EUR2.62), and 2026's to EUR33.50 (-EUR2.10), with strong Q3 results offset by weak guidance/bookings (EUR2.6B). Today, we maintain our 2024 and 2025 EPS views, but lift 2026's by EURO.65 to EUR34.15 to reflect greater confidence that activity in 2025 will be pushed into 2026, rather than downsized. We believe ASML's 2025 guide is sufficiently de-risked, including just 20% of sales from China (vs. 47% in Q3) to reflect negative sentiment (not action) on further export controls, along with very weak low-NA EUV sales as the successful foundries exercise caution in filling the market demand gap left by the laggards (i.e., Intel) despite strong Al momentum. Given this conservatism and ASML's multiple contraction, we see shares as significantly undervalued, especially given our view of strength in 2026. / Brooks 11:22 AM ET... [ASML We raise our price target by $156 to $1,169, 36x our '25 EPS view, above peers on ASML's significant market leadership in advanced lithography and above its three- year average (~28x) as Al momentum continues to grow alongside an emerging semiconductor upcycle. We lift our '24 EPS view by EURO.27 to EUR19.10 and increase '25's by EURO.44 to EUR30.34. ASML's multiple is high by historical standards, but we see this as justified. The company is a primary beneficiary of the Al wave given its unique ability to drive horizontal scaling via its near-monopoly position in advanced lithography, which helps enable the most advanced logic and memory chips. We are encouraged by recent announcements around High-NA EUV and expect the market for these machines to continue growing as Al demand broadens. CFRA Lowers Rating on Shares of ASML Holding N.V. to Buy from Strong Buy (ASML 951.68****): We raise our target price by $99 to $1,033, 32x our '25 EPS view, above peers on ASML's leading competitive position and sizeable backlog (EUR39B) and above its 3- year average (~29x) on accelerating demand for advanced equipment to support Al applications. We raise our '24 EPS view by EURO.36 to EUR18.76 and raise '25's by EURO.35 to EUR29.90 as our outlook for the semiconductor industry continues to broadly improve across key memory and logic applications. Our downgrade is valuation-based, as shares are up ~15% since the publishing of our previous note (Jan. 24) and up ~30% year-to-date. We believe shares have risen largely on a better-than-expected backlog position entering '24, with further support coming from strong peer earnings performance and positive industry commentary. Still, we think much of the near-term upside has been realized, and note that the focus in '24 is on building capacity to support '25 demand, while the potential for further China export restrictions remains a key risk, in our view. / Brooks Idlet, CFA Note: Research notes reflect CFRA's published opinions and analysis on the stock at the time the note was published. Glossary STARS Since January 1, 1987, CFRA Equity and Fund Research Services, and its predecessor S&P Capital IQ Equity Research has ranked a universe of U.S common stocks, ADRs (American Depositary Receipts), and ADSs (American Depositary Shares) based on a given equity's potential for future performance. Similarly, we have ranked Asian and European equities since June 30, 2002 Under proprietary STARS (Stock Appreciation Ranking System), equity analysts rank equities according to their individual forecast of an equity's future total return potential versus the expected total return of a relevant benchmark (e.g., a regional index (MSCI AC Asia Pacific Index, MSCI AC Europe Index or S&P 500\\U00ae Index]], based on a 12-month time horizon. STARS was designed to help investors looking to put their investment decisions in perspective. Data used to assist in determining the STARS ranking may be the result of the analyst's own models as well as internal proprietary models resulting from dynamic data inputs. S&P Global Market Intelligence's Quality Ranking (also known as S&P Capital IQ Earnings & Dividend Rankings) - Growth and SSP Capital IQ Earnings & Dividend Rankings stability of earnings and dividends are deemed key elements in establishing S&P Global Market Intelligence's earnings and dividend rankings for common stocks, which are designed to capsulize the nature of this record in a single symbol. It should be noted, however, that the process also takes into consideration certain adjustments and modifications deemed desirable in establishing such rankings. The final score for each stock is measured against a scoring matrix determined by analysis of the scores of a large and representative sample of stocks. The range of scores in the array of this sample has been aligned with the following ladder of rankings: A+ Highest High A Above B+ Average B Below Average B- Lower Lowest D In Reorganization NC Not Ranked EPS Estimates CFRA's earnings per share (EPS] estimates reflect analyst projections of future EPS from continuing operations, and generally exclude various items that are viewed as special, non-recurring, or extraordinary. Also, EPS estimates reflect either forecasts of equity analysts; or, the consensus (average) EPS estimate, which are independently compiled by S&P Global Market Intelligence, a data provider to CFRA. Among the items typically excluded from EPS estimates are asset sale gains; impairment, restructuring or merger-related charges; legal and insurance settlements; in process research and development expenses; gains or losses on the extinguishment of debt; the cumulative effect of accounting changes; and earnings related to operations that have been classified by the company as discontinued. The inclusion of some items, such 12-Month Target Price The equity analyst's projection of the market price a given security wil command 12 months hence, based on a combination of intrinsic, relative, and private market valuation metrics. CFRA Abbreviations Used in Equity Research Reports CAGR - Compound Annual Growth Rate CAPEX - Capital Expenditures CY - Calendar Year DCF - Discounted Cash Flow DDM - Dividend Discount Model EBIT - Earnings Before Interest and Taxes EBITDA - Earnings Before Interest, Taxes, Depreciation & Amortization EPS - Earnings Per Share EV - Enterprise Value FCF - Free Cash Flow FFO - Funds From Operations FY - Fiscal Year P/E - Price/Earnings P/NAV - Price to Net Asset Value PEG Ratio - P/E-to-Growth Ratio PV - Present Value R&D - Research & Development ROCE - Return on Capital Employed ROE Return on Equity ROl - Return on Investment ROIC - Return on Invested Capital ROA - Return on Assets SG&A - Selling, General & Administrative Expenses SOTP - Sum-of-The-Parts WACC - Weighted Average Cost of Capital Dividends on American Depository Receipts (ADRs) and American Depository Shares (ADSs) are net of taxes (paid in the country of origin). Reflects an equity analyst's view of a given company's operational risk, or the risk of a firm's ability to continue as an ongoing concern. The Qualitative Risk Assessment is a relative ranking to the U.S. STARS universe, and should be reflective of risk factors related to a company's operations, as opposed to risk and volatility measures associated with share prices. For an ETF this reflects on a capitalization-weighted basis, the average qualitative risk assessment assigned to holdings of the fund. STARS Ranking system and definition: **** * 5-STARS (Strong Buy): Total return is expected to outperform the total return of a relevant benchmark, by a notable margin over the coming 12 months, with shares rising in price on an absolute basis. **** * 4-STARS (Buy): Total return is expected to outperform the total return of a relevant benchmark over the coming 12 months. ** * * * 3-STARS (Hold): Total return is expected to closely approximate the total return of a relevant benchmark over the coming 12 months. ***** 2-STARS (Sell): Total return is expected to underperform the total return of a relevant benchmark over the coming 12 months. **** * 1-STAR (Strong Sell): Total return is expected to underperform the total return of a relevant benchmark by a notable margin over the coming 12 months, with shares falling in price on an absolute basis. Relevant benchmarks: In North America, the relevant benchmark is the S&P 500 Index, in Europe and in Asia, the relevant benchmarks are the MSCI AC Europe Index and the MSCI AC Asia Pacific Index, respectively. Disclosures Stocks are ranked in accordance with the following ranking methodologies: STARS Stock Reports: Qualitative STARS rankings are determined and assigned by equity analysts. For reports containing STARS rankings refer to the Glossary section of the report for detailed methodology and the definition of STARS rankings. Quantitative Stock Reports: Quantitative rankings are determined by ranking a universe of common stocks based on 5 measures or model categories: Valuation, Quality, Growth, Street Sentiment, and Price Momentum. In the U.S., a sixth sub-category for Financial Health will also be displayed. Percentile scores are used to compare each company to all other companies in the same universe for each model category. The five (six) model category scores are then weighted and rolled up into a single percentile ranking for that company. For reports containing quantitative rankings refer to the Glossary section seof the report for detailed methodology and the definition of Quantitative rankings. STARS Stock Reports and Quantitative Stock Reports: The methodologies used in STARS Stock Reports and Quantitative Stock Reports (collectively, the \\\"Research Reports\\\") reflect different criteria, assumptions and analytical methods and may have differing rankings. The methodologies and data used to generate the different types of Research Reports are believed by the author and distributor reasonable and appropriate. Generally, CFRA does not generate reports with different ranking methodologies for the same issuer. However, in the event that different methodologies or data are used on the analysis of an issuer, the methodologies may lead to different views on the issuer, which may at times result in contradicting assessments of an issuer. CFRA reserves the right to alter, replace or vary models, methodologies or assumptions from time to time and without notice to clients.
